Title: A Collection of Hymns, for the Use of the Protestant Church of the United Brethren
Creator: The Protestant Church of the United Brethren (Moravian Church); no personal editor named
Publisher: London: printed, and sold at the Brethren's Chapels in Great Britain and Ireland
Publication Date: 1789
Rights: Public Domain (published 1789, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item carries no rights field, so the date rule alone applies)
Source: Internet Archive item cbrethn00mora (https://archive.org/details/cbrethn00mora), the item's own cbrethn00mora_djvu.txt full-text OCR derivative; contributing library per the item metadata: Princeton Theological Seminary Library; host metadata date: 1789. Fetched directly (curl/urllib, raw bytes) by the Library thread (Era VIII pass), 2026-10-23. sha256 of the raw fetched bytes: c95280233d6900c8b1bece896a608450a8ce0de7f9d99f6b946d79a230060a6c.
Provenance note: Header prepended by the Library thread; nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, nothing retyped. Princeton Theological Seminary scan; readable with the long s partly rendered as f; a bequest plate precedes the title page and is left in place. The preface describes the 1754 and 1769 collections this one revises.
Content note: The revised English hymnal of 1789, trimmed from the 1754 collection as its own preface explains; the settled post-Zinzendorf repertoire the English congregations sang to the end of the window.

It  is  cur  ardent  vvlfh  and  fervent  prayer  unto  the  Lord*, 
that  he  may  render  this  publication  a  means  to  enliven  that

may  fjng  unto  him  with  humble  hearts  and  cheerful  voices,

voticn. 

Wcalfo  hope  that  our  endeavors,  however  im perfect,  will 
not  be  unacceptable  or  unprofitable  to  our  friends  and  other 
fincere  Chriftians,  who  according  to  the  advice  of  the  apoftle 
Paul,  (i  TheiT.  v.  21.)  "Prove  all  things,"  and  "hold  taft 
that  which  is  good."

fing,  though  their  voices  fhould  fometimes  jarr,  yet  the  har- 
mony of  fpirk  which  prevails  in  a  congregation  of  believers, 
will  prevent  in  a  great  meafure,  on  the  one  hand,  languor  in 
finging,  and,  on  the  other  hand,  exclude  unleemly  clamour

innumerable  'company  of  jult  men  made  perfect,  (Heb.

dreds and  tongues,  and  having  wafhed  their  robes  and  made
